Proof.

By summing over domains of action
X_{1}
and states
\bm{S}_{1}
, the interventional distribution
P_{\pi}\left(\bm{Y}\right)
for every policy
\pi\in\Pi
can be written as

\displaystyle P_{\pi}\left(\bm{y}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{1},\bm{s}_{1}}P_{\pi}\left(\bm{y}\mid x_{1},\bm{s}_{1}\right)P_{\pi}\left(x_{1}\mid\bm{s}_{1}\right)P_{\pi}\left(\bm{s}_{1}\right)

(558)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{1},\bm{s}_{1}}P_{x_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid\bm{s}_{1}\right)\pi_{1}(x_{1}\mid\bm{s}_{1})P_{\pi}\left(\bm{s}_{1}\right)

(559)

The last step holds since in submodel
\mathcal{M}^{*}_{\pi}
, values of
X_{1}
are determined by the decision rule
\pi_{1}(X_{1}\mid\bm{S}_{1})
. Note that states
\bm{S}_{1}
are non-descendants of actions
X_{1}\dots,X_{H}
in the manipulated diagram
\mathcal{G}_{\pi}
. The above equation can be further written as

\displaystyle P_{\pi}\left(\bm{y}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{1},\bm{s}_{1}}P_{x_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid\bm{s}_{1}\right)\pi_{1}(x_{1}\mid\bm{s}_{1})P(\bm{s}_{1})

(560)

Since the policy space
\Pi
is backdoor-admissible, this means that conditioning on nodes
\bm{S}_{1}
blocks all backdoor path from
X_{1}
to reward singals
\bm{Y}
in the manipulated diagram
\mathcal{G}_{\pi}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left(X_{1}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\bm{Y}\mid\bm{S}_{1}\right)_{\mathcal{G}_{\underline{X_{1}},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{H}}}

(561)

Applying Rule 2 of do-calculus in Def.
67
implies the following

\displaystyle P_{x_{1},\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid\bm{s}_{1}\right)=P_{\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid x_{1},\bm{s}_{1}\right)

(562)

Eqs.
560
and
562
imply

\displaystyle P_{\pi}\left(\bm{y}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{1},\bm{s}_{1}}P_{\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid x_{1},\bm{s}_{1}\right)\pi_{1}(x_{1}\mid\bm{s}_{1})P(\bm{s}_{1})

(563)

By repeatedly applying procedures for actions
X_{2},\dots,X_{H}
we obtain

\displaystyle P_{\pi}\left(\bm{y}\right)=\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}}P(\bm{y}\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})\prod_{i=1}^{H}P\left(s_{i}\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i-1}\right)\pi_{i}\left(x_{i}\mid\bm{s}_{i}\right)

(564)

The expected reward
\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\right]
of every policy
\pi\in\Pi
can thus be identified from the observational distribution
P(\bm{V})
and is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\right]=\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right]\prod_{i=1}^{H}P\left(s_{i}\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i-1}\right)\pi_{i}\left(x_{i}\mid\bm{s}_{i}\right)

(565)

The above identification formula is equivalent to Thms.
43
and
45
. Details of the derivations follow the discussion in Sec.
4.1.1
.
∎

Appendix I
Proofs for Chapter 5

See
75

Proof.

Recall that
N_{t}(x)
denotes the number of times an arm
x
was chosen up to episode
t
. The cumulative regret
\mathcal{R}(T,\mathcal{M}^{*})
could be decomposed as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(T,\mathcal{M}^{*})=\sum_{x\in\mathscr{D}(X)}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{E}\left[N_{T}(x)\right]

(566)

For any arm
x
, we upper bound
N_{T}(x)
on any sequence of interventions. We will discuss cases based on the causal upper bound
\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[Y\right]\leq r_{x}
.

If
r_{x}<\mu^{*}
.

In this case, the indicator function of
X^{(t)}=x
is bounded as follows

\displaystyle N_{T}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\left\{X^{(t)}=x\right\}

(567)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\left\{\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t}(x^{*},\delta)<\mu^{*}\right\}+\mathds{1}\left\{\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t}(x^{*},\delta)\geq\mu^{*},X^{(t)}=x\right\}

(568)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\left\{\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t}(x^{*},\delta)<\mu^{*}\right\}+\mathds{1}\left\{\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t}(x,\delta)\geq\mu^{*}\right\}

(569)

The last step holds since an arm
x
is played at time step
t
if and only if
\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t}(x,\delta)
is maximal. Recall that the clipped confidence bound is compatible with the causal bound
[l_{x},r_{x}]
, i.e.,

\displaystyle l_{x}\leq\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t}(x,\delta)\leq r_{x}

(570)

Since
r_{x}<\mu^{*}
, we must have
\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t}(x,\delta)<\mu^{*}
. Eq.
569
could thus be written as

\displaystyle N_{T}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\left\{\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t-1}(x^{*},\delta)<\mu^{*}\right\}

(571)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\left\{\text{UCB}_{t-1}(x^{*},\delta)<\mu^{*}\right\}

(572)

So we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[N_{T}(x)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}P\left(\text{UCB}_{{t-1}}(x^{*},\delta)<\mu^{*}\right)

(573)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}P\left(\hat{\mathbb{E}}^{(t-1)}_{x^{*}}[Y]+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{2N_{t-1}(x^{*})}}<\mu^{*}\right)

(574)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{2N_{t-1}(x^{*})=1}^{t}P\left(\hat{\mathbb{E}}^{(t-1)}_{x^{*}}[Y]+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{2N_{t-1}(x^{*})}}<\mu^{*}\right)

(575)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{2N_{t-1}(x^{*})=1}^{t}\delta

(576)

The last step follows Hoeffding’s inequalities (Eq.
181
). Since
\delta=t^{-4}
, we must have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[N_{T}(x)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{n}^{t-1}\frac{1}{t^{4}}

(577)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{t^{3}}

(578)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}

(579)

If
r_{x}\geq\mu^{*}
.

Let
l
be an arbitrary positive integer. The indicator function of
X^{(t)}=x
is bounded as

\displaystyle N_{T}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\left\{X^{(t)}=x\right\}

(580)

\displaystyle\leq l+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\left\{\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t-1}(x^{*},\delta)\leq\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t-1}(x,\delta),N_{t-1}(x)\geq l\right\}

(581)

Now observe that
\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t-1}(x^{*},\delta)\leq\overline{\text{UCB}}_{t-1}(x,\delta)
implies that at least one of the following events must hold true

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{x^{*}}^{(t-1)}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{2N_{t-1}(x^{*})}}\leq\mathbb{E}_{x^{*}}\left[Y\right]

(582)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{E}}_{x}^{(t-1)}-\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{2N_{t-1}(x)}}\geq\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[Y\right]

(583)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x^{*}}\left[Y\right]<\mathbb{E}_{x}\left[Y\right]+2\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{2N_{t-1}(x)}}

(584)

It follows from
(
8
, Theorem 1)
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[N_{T}(x)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\log(T)}{\Delta_{x}^{2}}+1+\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}

(585)

By applying Eqs.
576
and
579
, we bound the cumulative regret as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(T,\mathcal{M}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x:r_{x}<\mu^{*}}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{E}\left[N_{T}(x)\right]+\sum_{x:r_{x}\geq\mu^{*}}\Delta_{x}\mathbb{E}\left[N_{T}(x)\right]

(586)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x:r_{x}<\mu^{*}}\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\Delta_{x}+\sum_{x:r_{x}\geq\mu^{*}}\frac{8\log(T)}{\Delta_{x}}+\left(1+\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}\right)\Delta_{x}

(587)

Simplifying the above equation completes the proof.
∎

See
76

Proof.

The cumulative regret could be written as

\displaystyle R(T,\mathcal{M}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{*}}\left[\mathcal{R}{(\bm{Y})};\mathcal{M}^{*}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{R}\left(\bm{Y}^{(t)}\right)\right]

(588)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}\right]

(589)

Let
\epsilon\geq 0
be an arbitrary real value. The sum of performance gaps decomposes as follows

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\left\{\mathcal{M}^{*}\not\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\right\}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}\mathds{1}\left\{\mathcal{M}^{*}\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\right\}

(590)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\left\{\mathcal{M}^{*}\not\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\right\}}_{\text{Term 1}}

(591)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}\mathds{1}\left\{\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}\leq\epsilon,\mathcal{M}^{*}\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\right\}}_{\text{Term 2}}

(592)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}\mathds{1}\left\{\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}>\epsilon,\mathcal{M}^{*}\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\right\}}_{\text{Term 3}}

(593)

We will next bound the above terms separately.

Bounding Term 1.

By applying a union bound over all possible values of state-action counts
N_{t}(\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})
for every
i=1,\dots,H
, we obtain from Eq.
278

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}P\left(\mathcal{M}^{*}\not\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{n=1}^{t-1}P\left(\mathcal{M}^{*}\not\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\right)

(594)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{n=1}^{t-1}\delta

(595)

Since
\delta=t^{-4}
, the above equation could be further written as

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}P\left(\mathcal{M}^{*}\not\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\right)\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{n=1}^{t-1}\frac{1}{t^{4}}\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}

(596)

Bounding Term 2.

Observe that the algorithm only selects deterministic policies in
\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}
. This implies that for any policy
\pi^{(t)}
such that
\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}\leq\epsilon
, its performance gap is bounded by that of the worst deterministic policy
\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}
with
\Delta_{\pi}\leq\epsilon
. We thus have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}P\left(\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}\leq\epsilon,\mathcal{M}^{*}\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\right)\leq\max_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}:\Delta_{\pi}\leq\epsilon}\Delta{\pi}T

(597)

Bounding Term 3.

Let
\Delta(\epsilon,T)
denote Term 3. Since
\mathcal{M}^{*}\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)
, the optimistic planning in Eq.
301
implies

\displaystyle\Delta(\epsilon,T)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{*}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y});\mathcal{M}^{*}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{(t)}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y});\mathcal{M}^{*}\right]\right)\mathds{1}\left\{\mathcal{M}^{*}\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\right\}

(598)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{(t)}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y});\mathcal{M}^{(t)}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{(t)}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y});\mathcal{M}^{*}\right]

(599)

For convenience, we will use the superscript
P^{(t)}
to denote a distribution evaluated in SCM
\mathcal{M}^{(t)}
and
P
for a distribution evaluated in the true SCM
\mathcal{M}^{*}
. Fix a policy
\pi\in\Pi
. For every
i=0,\dots,H
, define
V^{(i)}_{\pi}(\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})
as a function given by

\displaystyle V^{(i)}_{\pi}=\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}}\mathbb{E}^{(t)}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right]

\displaystyle\prod_{j=i}^{H-1}P^{(t)}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{j}}\left[s_{j+1}\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right]\pi_{j+1}\left(x_{j+1}\mid\bm{s}_{j+1}\right)

(600)

\displaystyle\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{j}}\left(s_{j+1}\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}\right)\pi_{j+1}\left(x_{j+1}\mid\bm{s}_{j+1}\right)

(601)

and define
V^{(H+1)}_{\pi}=\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y});\mathcal{M}^{*}\right]
. Observe that
V^{(0)}_{\pi}=\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y});\mathcal{M}^{(t)}\right]
. Eq.
599
can thus be written as a telescoping sum

\displaystyle\Delta(\epsilon,T)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=0}^{H}V^{(i)}_{\pi^{(t)}}-\bar{V}^{(i+1)}_{\pi^{(t)}}

(602)

For every
i=0,\dots,H-1
,

\displaystyle V^{(i)}_{\pi^{(t)}}-V^{(i+1)}_{\pi^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}}\left\lVert P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}\left(\cdot\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{i};\mathcal{M}^{(t)}\right)-P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}\left(\cdot\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{i};\mathcal{M}^{*}\right)\right\rVert_{1}

(603)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{2\left|\mathscr{D}(\bm{S}_{i+1})\right|\log(4H\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{i}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{i})\right|/\delta)}\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{t}\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}\right)}}

(604)

and similarly,

\displaystyle V^{(H)}_{\pi^{(t)}}-V^{(H+1)}_{\pi^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H};\mathcal{M}^{(t)}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H};\mathcal{M}^{*}\right]\right\rvert

(605)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\log(8H\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{H})\right|/\delta)}\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{t}\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right)}}

(606)

Eqs.
604
and
606
imply

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=0}^{H}V^{(i)}_{\pi^{(t)}}-\bar{V}^{(i+1)}_{\pi^{(t)}}

(607)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{H-1}2\sqrt{2\left|\mathscr{D}(\bm{S}_{i+1})\right|\log(4H\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{i}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{i})\right|/\delta)}\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{t}\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}\right)}}

(608)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{2\log(8H\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{H})\right|/\delta)}\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2N_{t}\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right)}}

(609)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{H-1}4\sqrt{2\left|\mathscr{D}(\bm{S}_{i+1})\right|\log(4H\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{i}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{i})\right|/\delta)}\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}}\sqrt{N_{T}\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}\right)}

(610)

\displaystyle+2\sqrt{2\log(8H\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{H})\right|/\delta)}\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}}\sqrt{N_{T}\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right)}

(611)

The last step follows from
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}<2\sqrt{n}-1
. Eq.
602
could thus be written as

\displaystyle\Delta(\epsilon,T)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{H-1}4\sqrt{2\left|\mathscr{D}(\bm{S}_{i+1})\right|\log(4H\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{i}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{i})\right|/\delta)}\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}}\sqrt{N_{T}\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}\right)}

(612)

\displaystyle+2\sqrt{2\log(8H\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{H})\right|/\delta)}\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}}\sqrt{N_{T}\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right)}

(613)

Let
T_{\epsilon}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\left\{\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}>\epsilon\right\}
. Applying Jensen’s inequality gives

\displaystyle\Delta(\epsilon,T)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{H-1}4\sqrt{2\left|\mathscr{D}(\bm{S}_{i+1})\right|\log(4H\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{i}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{i})\right|/\delta)}\sqrt{\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{i}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{i})\right|T_{\epsilon}}

(614)

\displaystyle+2\sqrt{2\log(8H\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{H})\right|/\delta)}\sqrt{\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{H})\right|T_{\epsilon}}

(615)

Let
\delta=t^{-4}
. A few simplifications give

\displaystyle\Delta(\epsilon,T)\leq 17H\sqrt{T_{\epsilon}\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{H})\right|\log(T)}

(616)

Observe that the algorithm only selects deterministic policies in
\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}
. This implies that for any policy
\pi^{(t)}
such that
\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}>\epsilon
, its performance gap is lower bounded by that of the best possible deterministic policy
\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}
with
\Delta_{\pi}>\epsilon
. This gives

\displaystyle\min_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}:\Delta_{\pi}>\epsilon}\Delta_{\pi}T_{\epsilon}\leq\Delta(\epsilon,T)

(617)

Eqs.
616
and
617
imply

\displaystyle T_{\epsilon}\leq\max_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}:\Delta_{\pi}>\epsilon}\frac{17^{2}H^{2}\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{H})\right|\log(T)}{\Delta^{2}_{\pi}}

(618)

Note that
\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}=\bm{S}
and
\bar{\bm{X}}_{H}=\bm{X}
. Eqs.
616
and
618
imply

\displaystyle\Delta(\epsilon,T)\leq\max_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}:\Delta_{\pi}>\epsilon}\frac{17^{2}H^{2}\left|\mathscr{D}(\bm{S}\cup\bm{X})\right|\log(T)}{\Delta_{\pi}}

(619)

By Eqs.
596
,
597
and
619
, we have

\displaystyle R(T,\mathcal{M}^{*})\leq\max_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}:\Delta_{\pi}>\epsilon}\frac{17^{2}H^{2}\left|\mathscr{D}(\bm{S}\cup\bm{X})\right|\log(T)}{\Delta_{\pi}}+\max_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}:\Delta_{\pi}\leq\epsilon}\Delta{\pi}T+\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}

(620)

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 147
.

Let
\langle\mathcal{M}^{*},\Pi,\mathcal{R}\rangle
be a CDM where the policy space
\Pi=\left\{\langle X_{i},\bm{S}_{i}\rangle\right\}_{X_{i}\in\bm{X}}
. For every
i=1,\dots,H-1
,

\displaystyle P_{{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}}\left(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1}\right)-P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}\left(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}\right)\leq P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}).

(621)

Proof.

For any set of variables
\bm{V}
, let
\neg\bm{v}
denote an event
\bm{V}\neq\bm{v}
. Note that
P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}\left(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1}\right)
can be written as the counterfactual distribution
P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}})
. We thus have

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}\left(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1}\right)=P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\neg x_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\neg\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1}),

(622)

By the composition axiom (Thm.
146
),
\bar{\bm{S}}_{i+1_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}}(\bm{u})=\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1}
if
\bar{\bm{X}}_{i}(\bm{u})=\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}
. So,

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}\left(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1}\right)

\displaystyle=P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\neg x_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\neg\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1})

\displaystyle\leq P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\neg x_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\neg\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1})

\displaystyle=P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1})

\displaystyle=P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})

\displaystyle=P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}).

The last step holds sine
P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}})=P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})
. Since
\bar{\bm{S}}_{i_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}}(\bm{u})=\bar{\bm{S}}_{i}
if
\bar{\bm{X}}_{i}(\bm{u})=\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}
, applying the composition axiom (Thm.
146
) again gives

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}\left(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1}\right)\leq P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})

(623)

Rearranging the above equation completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 148
.

Let
\langle\mathcal{M}^{*},\Pi,\mathcal{R}\rangle
be a CDM where the policy space
\Pi=\left\{\langle X_{i},\bm{S}_{i}\rangle\right\}_{X_{i}\in\bm{X}}
. For every
i=1,\dots,H-1
,
P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}\left(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1}\right)\leq\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})
where
\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})
is a function defined in Eq.
284
.

Proof.

We will prove this statement by induction.

Base Case:
i=0

By definition,
\Gamma(s_{1})=P(s_{1})
. We thus have
P(s_{1})\leq\Gamma(s_{1})
.

Induction Step

Assume that the statement holds for
k
, i.e.,
P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1})\leq\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})
. We will next show that the statement holds for
i+1
, i.e.,

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1}}\left(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+2}\right)\leq\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+2},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1})

(624)

To begin with,

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+2})=P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+2})-P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1})+P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1}).

(625)

Applying Lem.
147
gives

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+2})

\displaystyle\leq P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+2},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1})+P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1})

(626)

\displaystyle\leq P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+2},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1})+P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1})

(627)

The last step holds since
\bar{\bm{S}}_{i+1}
are non-descendants of
X_{i+1}
. By the induction assumption,
P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1})\leq\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})
. This implies

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+2})

\displaystyle\leq P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+2},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1})+\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})

(628)

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+2},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i+1}).

(629)

∎

See
77

Proof.

By the definition of the conditional distribution,

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(s_{i+1}|\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})={P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1})\over P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})}.

(630)

Applying Lem.
147
gives:

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(s_{k+1}|\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})

\displaystyle\leq 1+{P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})\over P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})}

(631)

\displaystyle\leq 1+{P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})\over P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})}

(632)

The last step holds since
\bar{\bm{S}}_{i}
are non-descendants of action
X_{i}
.
Among the above quantities, the numerator
P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})\leq 0
, since the marginal probability is not larger than its joint. Therefore, the above upper bound is maximized when the denominator
P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})
is the maximal. By applying Lem.
148
, we obtain the upper bound

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(s_{k+1}|\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})

\displaystyle\leq 1+{P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})\over\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1})}

(633)

\displaystyle\leq{\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})\over\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1})}.

(634)

We will now derive the lower bound. By the definition of counterfactual distributions (Def.
144
),

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1})=P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{{i+1}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}})

(635)

By expanding over the domain of observed actions
\bar{\bm{X}}_{i}
,

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(s_{k+1}|\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})

\displaystyle={P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{{i+1}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})+P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{{i+1}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\neg\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})\over P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})}

(636)

\displaystyle\geq{P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{{i+1}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})\over P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})}.

(637)

Since
\bar{\bm{S}}_{i+1_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}}(\bm{u})=\bar{\bm{S}}_{i+1}
if
\bar{\bm{X}}_{i}(\bm{u})=\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}
, the composition axiom (Thm.
146
) implies

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(s_{k+1}|\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})\geq{P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})\over P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})}

(638)

By applying Lem.
148
again, we obtain the lower bound

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{i}}(s_{k+1}|\bar{\bm{s}}_{i})\geq{P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i+1},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i})\over\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{i},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1})}.

(639)

This completes the proof.
∎

See
79

Proof.

By basic probabilistic operations,

\displaystyle E_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}[Y\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}]={E_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}]P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})\over P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})}

(640)

Note that the reward function
\mathcal{R}(\bm{y})\in[0,1]
. By following a similar argument as Lem.
147
, we could show that

\displaystyle E_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right]P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})-P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})\leq E\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}\right]P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H})-P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}).

(641)

Rearranging Eq.
641
gives

\displaystyle E_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1-{\left(1-E\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}\right]\right)P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H})\over P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})}

(642)

\displaystyle\leq 1-{\left(1-E\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}\right]\right)P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H})\over P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H-1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})}

(643)

The last step holds since
\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}
are non-descendants of
X_{H}
. Since
E\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}\right]\leq 1
, the above equation is maximized when
P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})
is the maximal. Applying Lem.
148
gives the upper bound

\displaystyle E_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right]\leq 1-{\left(1-E\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}\right]\right)P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H})\over\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{k},\bar{\bm{x}}_{k-1})}.

(644)

We will next derive the lower bound. By the definition of counterfactual distributions (Def.
144
),

\displaystyle P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}(\bm{y},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})=P(\bm{y}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}},\bar{\bm{s}}_{K_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H-1}}})=P(\bm{y}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}},\bar{\bm{s}}_{K_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}})

(645)

The last step holds since
\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}
are non-descendants of
X_{H}
. By basic probabilistic operations,

\displaystyle E_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{\bm{y}}\mathcal{R}(\bm{y})P\left(\bm{y}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H-1}}}\right)}{P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H-1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})}

(646)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sum_{\bm{y}}\mathcal{R}(\bm{y})P\left(\bm{y}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H-1}}},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}\right)}{P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H-1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})}

(647)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sum_{\bm{y}}\mathcal{R}(\bm{y})P\left(\bm{y},\bar{\bm{s}},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}\right)}{P_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H-1}}(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H})}

(648)

The last follows from the composition axiom (Thm.
146
),
\bm{Y}_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}(\bm{u})=\bm{Y}(\bm{u}),\bar{\bm{S}}_{H_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H-1}}}(\bm{u})=\bar{\bm{S}}_{H-1}
if
\bar{\bm{X}}_{H}(\bm{u})=\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}
. Applying Lem.
148
gives the lower bound

\displaystyle E_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sum_{\bm{y}}\mathcal{R}(\bm{y})P\left(\bm{y},\bar{\bm{s}},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}\right)}{\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H-1})}

(649)

\displaystyle={E[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H}]P(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H})\over\Gamma(\bar{\bm{s}}_{H},\bar{\bm{x}}_{H-1})}

(650)

This completes the proof.
∎

See
81

Proof.

Let
\epsilon\geq 0
be an arbitrary real value. The sum of performance gaps is bounded as follows.

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\left\{\mathcal{M}^{*}\not\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\cap\mathbb{M}_{c}\right\}}_{\text{Term 1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}\mathds{1}\left\{\mathcal{M}^{*}\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\cap\mathbb{M}_{c}\right\}}_{\text{Term 2}}

(651)

Among the quantities in the above equation, Term 1 is bounded following Eq.
596
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}P\left(\mathcal{M}^{*}\not\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\cap\mathbb{M}_{c}\right)\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}P\left(\mathcal{M}^{*}\not\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\right)\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}

(652)

Let
T_{0}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathds{1}\{\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}>0\}
be the total number of episodes where a suboptimal policy is picked. Let
\Delta(T)
denote Term 2, which could be bounded following Eq.
616

\displaystyle\Delta(T)\leq 17H\sqrt{T_{0}\left|\mathscr{D}(\bar{\bm{S}}_{H}\cup\bar{\bm{X}}_{H})\right|\log(T)}

(653)

Observe that when
\mathcal{M}^{*}\in\mathbb{M}_{t-1}(\delta)\cap\mathbb{M}_{c}
, it follows from the optimistic planning in Eq.
301
that a suboptimal policy
\pi
with
r_{\pi}<\mu^{*}
is never selected. This gives

\displaystyle\Delta(T)\geq\min_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}:\Delta_{\pi}>0,r_{\pi}\geq\mu^{*}}\Delta_{\pi}T_{0}

(654)

Eqs.
653
and
654

\displaystyle T_{0}\leq\max_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}:\Delta_{\pi}>\epsilon,r_{\pi}\geq\mu^{*}}\frac{17^{2}H^{2}\left|\mathscr{D}(\bm{S}\cup\bm{X})\right|\log(T)}{\Delta^{2}_{\pi}}

(655)

Eqs.
653
and
655
imply

\displaystyle\Delta(T)\leq\max_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}:\Delta_{\pi}>\epsilon,r_{\pi}\geq\mu^{*}}\frac{17^{2}H^{2}\left|\mathscr{D}(\bm{S}\cup\bm{X})\right|\log(T)}{\Delta_{\pi}}

(656)

By Eqs.
652
and
656
, we have

\displaystyle R(T,\mathcal{M}^{*})=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Delta_{\pi^{(t)}}\right]\leq\max_{\pi\in\Pi^{\mathrm{o}}:\Delta_{\pi}>\epsilon,r_{\pi}\geq\mu^{*}}\frac{17^{2}H^{2}\left|\mathscr{D}(\bm{S}\cup\bm{X})\right|\log(T)}{\Delta_{\pi}}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}

(657)

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix J
Proofs for Chapter 8

See
120

Proof.

Since the NUC condition holds, applying the IPW formula in Thm.
43
implies, for any policy
\pi\in\Pi
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\right]=\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}}E\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right]P\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{H}\frac{\pi_{i}\left(x_{i}\mid\bm{s}_{i}\right)}{P\left(x_{i}\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}\right)}

(658)

Since the local Markov property holds, we must have

\displaystyle P\left(x_{i}\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{s}}_{i}\right)=P\left(x_{i}\mid\bm{s}_{i}\right)

(659)

Eqs.
658
and
659
imply

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\right]=\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}}E\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right]P\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{H}\frac{\pi_{i}\left(x_{i}\mid\bm{s}_{i}\right)}{P\left(x_{i}\mid\bm{s}_{i}\right)}

(660)

Let policy
\pi
be such that
\pi_{i}(X_{i}\mid\bm{S}_{i})=P(X_{i}\mid\bm{S}_{i})
for every
i=1,\dots,H
. The expected reward of
\pi
could be further written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}}E\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right]P\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{H},\bar{\bm{s}}_{H}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{H}\frac{P\left(x_{i}\mid\bm{s}_{i}\right)}{P\left(x_{i}\mid\bm{s}_{i}\right)}

(661)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{R}(\bm{Y})\right]

(662)

That is, behavioral cloning perfectly imitates the expert’s performance.
∎

See
127

Proof.

We first show the sufficiency of the imitation backdoor criterion (Def.
123
). Consider the following claim, for every
i=1,\dots,H
,

\displaystyle P_{\pi_{i},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{Y}\right)=P_{\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{Y}\right)

(663)

If
X_{i}\not\in\mathit{An}(\bm{Y})
is not an ancestor of nodes in
\bm{Y}
in the manipulated diagram
\mathcal{G}_{\pi_{i},\dots,\pi_{H}}
, Eq.
663
trivially holds. We now consider the second condition,
X_{i}\in\mathit{An}(\bm{Y})
in
\mathcal{G}_{\pi_{i},\dots,\pi_{H}}
. By conditioning on action
X_{i}
and covariates
\bm{Z}_{i}
,

\displaystyle P_{\pi_{i},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{i},\bm{z}_{i}}P_{x_{i},\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y},\bm{z}_{i}\right)\pi_{i}(x_{i}\mid\bm{z}_{i})

(664)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{i},\bm{z}_{i}}P_{x_{i},\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid\bm{z}_{i}\right)\pi_{i}(x_{i}\mid\bm{z}_{i})P(\bm{z}_{i})

(665)

The last step holds since
\bm{Z}_{i}
are non-descendants of actions
X_{i},\dots,X_{H}
in the manipulated diagram
\mathcal{G}_{\pi_{i},\dots,\pi_{H}}
. Since
\Pi^{\prime}
is imitation admissible, covariates
\bm{Z}_{i}
block all backdoor paths from node
X_{i}
to
\bm{Y}
in
\mathcal{G}_{\pi_{i},\dots,\pi_{H}}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left(\bm{Y}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}X_{i}\mid\bm{Z}_{i}\right)_{\mathcal{G}_{\underline{X_{i}},\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}}

(666)

Applying Rule 2 of do-calculus (Def.
67
) implies

\displaystyle P_{x_{i},\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid\bm{z}_{i}\right)=P_{\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid x_{i},\bm{z}_{i}\right)

(667)

By Eqs.
663
and
667
we have

\displaystyle P_{\pi_{i},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{i},\bm{z}_{i}}P_{\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid x_{i},\bm{z}_{i}\right)\pi_{i}(x_{i}\mid\bm{z}_{i})P(\bm{z}_{i})

(668)

Let the decision rule
\pi_{i}(X_{i}\mid\bm{Z}_{i})=P(X_{i}\mid\bm{Z}_{i})
. The above equation could be written as

\displaystyle P_{\pi_{i},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{i},\bm{z}_{i}}P_{\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid x_{i},\bm{z}_{i}\right)P(x_{i}\mid\bm{z}_{i})P(\bm{z}_{i})

(669)

\displaystyle=P_{\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\right)

(670)

Now, repeatedly applying Eq.
663
for every
i=1,\dots,H
gives

\displaystyle P_{\pi}\left(\bm{y}\right)=P_{\pi_{1},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\right)=P_{\pi_{2},\dots,\pi_{H}}\left(\bm{y}\right)=\cdots=P(\bm{y})

(671)

That is, policy
\pi
perfectly imitates the expert’s performance.
∎

See
135

Proof.

Note that
\pi
is a
mixed policy
which is a probability distribution over deterministic policies compatible with space
\Pi^{\prime}
. With a slight abuse of notation, we will treat
\pi
as a vector where
\pi(i)
is the probability assigned to the
i
-th deterministic policy
\pi^{(i)}
. The claimed statement follows immediately from the definition of the game matrix
\bm{G}
.
∎

See
136

Proof.

Let the vector
\alpha=\rho-\rho_{\bm{\pi}}
. The claimed statement follows from the definition of the conjugate function
\psi^{*}
.
∎

See
133

Proof.

For any
i=1,\dots,N
, let
\pi_{i:H}
be a sequence of decision rules
\{\pi_{i},\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}\}
. It is sufficient to show that for any
i=1,\dots,n
,

\displaystyle P_{\pi_{i:H}}\left(\bm{y},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i-1}\right)=\sum_{x_{i},\bm{z}_{i}}P_{\pi_{i+1:H}}\left(\bm{y},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i}\right)\frac{\pi_{i}(x_{i}\mid\bm{z}_{i})}{P(x_{i}\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i})}

(672)

By basic probabilistic operations,

\displaystyle P_{\pi_{i:H}}\left(\bm{y},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{i},\bm{z}_{i}}P_{\pi_{i:H}}\left(\bm{y},\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i}\right)

(673)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{i},\bm{z}_{i}}P_{\pi_{i:H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i}\right)P_{\pi_{i:H}}\left(x_{i}\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i}\right)P_{\pi_{i:H}}\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i}\right)

(674)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{i},\bm{z}_{i}}P_{x_{i},\pi_{i+1:H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i}\right)\pi_{i}(x_{i}\mid\bm{z}_{i})P(\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i})

(675)

In the last step,
P_{\pi_{i:H}}\left(\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i}\right)=P(\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i})
since every causal diagram
\mathcal{G}_{\pi_{i:H}}
is acyclic, and
\bar{\bm{X}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{Z}}_{i}
are non-descendants of actions
X_{i},\dots,X_{H}
. Next, we will show that

\displaystyle P_{x_{i},\pi_{i+1:H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i}\right)=P_{\pi_{i+1:H}}\left(\bm{y}\mid\bar{\bm{x}}_{i},\bar{\bm{z}}_{i}\right)

(676)

This is equivalent to showing that

\displaystyle(\bm{Y}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}X_{i}|\bar{\bm{X}}_{i-1},\bar{\bm{Z}}_{i})\text{ in }\mathcal{G}_{\underline{X_{i}},\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}

(677)

First, by definition of imitation backdoor,

\displaystyle(\bm{Y}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 3.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}X_{i}|\bm{Z}_{i})\text{ in }\mathcal{G}_{\underline{X_{i}},\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}

(678)

Suppose Eq.
677
does not hold. We must have a node
W\in\bar{\bm{Z}}_{i-1}\cup\{X_{i}\}
opening a collider path from
X_{i}
to
\bm{Y}
. Assume that there exists a directed path from
W
to
\bm{Y}
in the manipulated diagram
\mathcal{G}_{\underline{X_{i}},\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}
. Then one could construct a backdoor path from node
X_{i}
to a node in
\bm{Y}
via
W
, which contradicts the independence relationship in Eq.
678
.

What remains is to show that there must exist a directed path from
W
to
\bm{Y}
in the manipulated diagram
\mathcal{G}_{\underline{X_{i}},\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}
. Since
\Pi^{\prime}
is a minimal imitation admissible, it is verifiable from
(
196
, Lem. 3.4)
that
W\in\mathit{An}(\bm{Y})
in
\mathcal{G}_{\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}
. Consequently, the only case that
W\not\in\mathit{An}(\bm{Y})
in
\mathcal{G}_{\underline{X_{i}},\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}
is when
W\in\mathit{An}(X_{i})
in
\mathcal{G}_{\pi_{i+1},\dots,\pi_{H}}
. Again, this allows us to construct a backdoor path between node
X_{i}
and nodes in
\bm{Y}
via node
W
